breakdown: 
meaning: oh, ah
notes: Expresses a sigh or a feeling of regret/pity here.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: Means 'too much' or 'excessively,' but colloquially it's often used simply to mean 'very' or 'really,' especially with positive adjectives. Here, it emphasizes the degree of regret.
--- word ---
term: 아쉽다
type: adjective
breakdown: 
meaning: it's a pity, it's regrettable
notes: Describes a feeling of regret, disappointment, or a sense of loss for something that could have been better or that is ending. It's often used when something is 'missed' or 'lacking.' Informal plain form.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: Repetition for emphasis and to transition to the next thought, indicating a consequence of the regret.

=== [30:06] 되게 기도 드리듯이 인사를 하고 왔어 ===
meaning: So I said goodbye as if I were praying.
tone: Informal, slightly dramatic, sentimental.
--- word ---
term: 되게
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: very, really
notes: An informal adverb meaning 'very' or 'really,' similar to '정말' (jeongmal) or '아주' (aju). It intensifies the following adjective or verb. Common in spoken Korean.
--- word ---
term: 기도
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: prayer
notes: The noun for 'prayer.' Often combined with 하다 (hada) to form the verb '기도하다' (gidohada, 'to pray').
--- word ---
term: 드리듯이
type: verb ending
breakdown: 드리다 + -듯이
meaning: as if offering (a prayer), as if praying
notes: 드리다 (deurida) is the honorific form of 주다 (juda, 'to give'), often used in the context of offering something respectful, like a prayer. -듯이 (-deusi) is a conjunctive ending meaning 'as if,' 'as though,' or 'like.' It indicates that the action in the main clause is performed in a manner similar to the preceding clause. Here, it means 'as if offering a prayer.'
--- word ---
term: 인사를
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 인사 + 를
meaning: greeting (object)
notes: 인사 (insa) means 'greeting' or 'bow.' 를 (reul) is the object particle, indicating that 'greeting' is the direct object of the verb.
--- word ---
term: 하고
type: verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + -고
meaning: doing and
notes: 하다 (hada) means 'to do.' -고 (-go) is a conjunctive ending that connects two or more actions in chronological order or lists them. Here, it means 'did [the greeting] and then [came].'
--- word ---
term: 왔어
type: verb
breakdown: 오다 + -았어
meaning: came
notes: 오다 (oda) means 'to come.' -았어 (-asseo) is the informal past tense ending. The full form is 왔어요 (watsseoyo).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-듯이
meaning: Used to indicate that an action is performed in a manner similar to or as if the preceding clause were true. It means 'as if,' 'as though,' or 'like'.
example: 비 오듯이 땀을 흘렸다. (Sweat poured down as if it were raining.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 V
meaning: Connects two or more verbs, indicating that the actions occur in sequence or are listed. It means 'and then' or 'and'.
example: 밥을 먹고 영화를 봤어. (I ate and then watched a movie.)
level: Beginner

=== [30:10] 그랬는데 그 다음 집에 적응을 너무 빨리 잘하고 있는 기분이야 지금 ===

--- word ---
term: 그

term: 적응을
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 적응 + 을
meaning: adaptation (object)
notes: 적응 (jeogeung) means 'adaptation' or 'adjustment.' 을 (eul) is the object particle. The verb is 적응하다 (jeogeunghada, 'to adapt').
--- word ---
term: 너무

breakdown: 
meaning: too, very
notes: Used to intensify the following adverb '빨리' (ppalli, 'quickly').

type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: quickly
notes: An adverb meaning 'quickly' or 'fast.' Derived from 빠르다 (ppareuda, 'to be fast').
--- word ---
term: 잘하고
type: verb
breakdown: 잘하다 + -고
meaning: doing well and
notes: 잘하다 (jalhada) means 'to do well.' -고 (-go) is a conjunctive ending connecting 'doing well' with 'being' (있는). It indicates simultaneous or sequential actions.
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: verb
breakdown: 있다 + -는
meaning: being, existing (adjectival form)
notes: 있다 (itda) means 'to be' or 'to exist.' -는 (-neun) is an adjectival ending for verbs, indicating an ongoing action or state. It modifies '기분' (gibun, 'feeling'). So, 'the feeling of being [in the process of] doing well.'
--- word ---
term: 기분이야
type: noun + verb
breakdown: 기분 + 이야
meaning: it's a feeling
notes: 기분 (gibun) means 'feeling' or 'mood.' 이야 (iya) is the informal declarative ending for nouns, meaning 'it is' or 'it's.' It's the casual form of 입니다 (imnida) or 이에요/예요 (ieyo/yeyo).
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now
notes: An adverb meaning 'now' or 'at present.' It indicates the current time. Compare with 이제 (ije), which means 'from now on' or 'at this point in time' (a new phase).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-았/었는데
meaning: Connects two clauses, indicating a contrast, background information, or an unexpected result. It means 'but,' 'however,' or 'although'.
example: 밥을 먹었는데 아직 배고파. (I ate, but I'm still hungry.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 있다
meaning: Indicates an ongoing action or state, similar to the English present continuous tense ('-ing').
example: 책을 읽고 있어요. (I am reading a book.)
level: Beginner

=== [30:19] 그래서 좀 전 집한테 좀 미안할 정도로 ===

--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: Connects to the previous statement, indicating a consequence or result.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a little, somewhat
notes: A common adverb meaning 'a little' or 'somewhat.' It softens a statement or indicates a slight degree. Here, it softens the feeling of 'sorry.'
--- word ---
term: 전
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: former, previous
notes: A prefix or determiner meaning 'former,' 'previous,' or 'ex-.' Here, it modifies '집' (jip, 'house') to mean 'former house' or 'old house.'
--- word ---
term: 집한테
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 집 + 한테
meaning: to the house, towards the house
notes: 집 (jip) means 'house.' 한테 (hante) is a particle meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. Colloquially, it can sometimes be used with inanimate objects when personifying them or expressing a feeling directed towards them, as Hwasa does here with her old house, implying a personal relationship with it. The more standard particle for inanimate objects would be 에 (e) or 에게 (ege).
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a little, somewhat
notes: Repetition of '좀' for emphasis, further softening the expression of guilt.

--- word ---
term: 정도로
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 정도 + 로
meaning: to the extent of
notes: 정도 (jeongdo) means 'degree,' 'extent,' or 'level.' -(으)로 (euro/ro) is a particle indicating means, method, or standard. Combined, '정도로' means 'to the extent that' or 'to the degree of.' It's a common pattern to express the intensity of a feeling or action.
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V-ㄹ/을 정도로
meaning: Used to express the degree or extent of an action or state, meaning 'to the extent that...' or 'so... that...'.
example: 눈물이 날 정도로 슬펐어. (I was so sad that tears came.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: N한테
meaning: A particle meaning 'to' or 'from' a person or animal. Colloquially, it can sometimes be used with inanimate objects when personifying them.
example: 친구한테 전화했어. (I called my friend.)
level: Beginner

=== [30:22] 하이라이터 뭐 쓰냐고? 몰라 ===

breakdown: 
meaning: what
notes: An interrogative pronoun meaning 'what.' Used here in a question about the type of highlighter.
--- word ---

type: verb ending
breakdown: 쓰다 + -냐고
meaning: asking if I use
notes: 쓰다 (sseuda) means 'to use.' -냐고 (-nyago) is an informal indirect question ending, meaning 'asking if...' or 'saying that [someone] asks if....' It's a contraction of -냐고 하다 (nyago hada). meaning: Used for indirect questions, meaning 'asking if...' or 'saying that someone asks if...'. It's an informal way to report a question.
example: 뭐 하냐고 물어봤어. (I asked what he was doing.)

=== [30:31] 이건 뭐랄까 하이라이터만의 문제는 아닌 거 같은데 ===

term: 문제는
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 문제 + 는
meaning: problem (topic)

term: 아닌
type: adjective
breakdown: 아니다 + -ㄴ
meaning: not being (adjectival form)
notes: 아니다 (anida) means 'to not be.' -ㄴ (-n) is an adjectival ending that modifies a following noun, indicating a negative state or identity. Here, it modifies '거' (geo, 'thing/fact').

term: 거
type: contraction
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: Contraction of 것 (geot, 'thing' or 'fact'). Used here to nominalize the preceding clause, meaning 'the fact that it's not.'
--- word ---
term: 같은데
type: adjective + ending
breakdown: 같다 + -은데
meaning: it seems like, but
notes: 같다 (gatda) means 'to be like' or 'to seem.' -은데 (-eunde) is a conjunctive ending that provides background information, expresses mild surprise, or sets up a contrast. Here, it means 'it seems like [that], but...' or 'I think [that], so....' It often implies a continuation of thought or a slight reservation. Informal and very common in spoken Korean.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N만
meaning: Attaches to nouns to mean 'only' or 'just' that noun, emphasizing exclusivity.
example: 너만 좋아해. (I only like you.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V-ㄴ/은/는 것 같다
meaning: Used to express conjecture or opinion, meaning 'it seems like,' 'it looks like,' or 'I think that....' -ㄴ/은 is for adjectives/past verbs, -는 for present verbs.
example: 비가 오는 것 같아요. (It seems like it's raining.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V-ㄴ/은/는데
meaning: A versatile conjunctive ending that provides background information, expresses mild surprise, or sets up a contrast. It can mean 'but,' 'and,' 'so,' or 'since'.
example: 날씨가 좋은데 산책할까요? (The weather is nice, so shall we take a walk?)
level: Beginner

=== [30:46] 기분 나쁠래 그래? ===
meaning: Are you trying to make me upset?
tone: Informal, teasing, rhetorical.
--- word ---
term: 기분
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: feeling, mood
notes: Refers to one's emotional state or mood. Often used with verbs like 좋다 (jota, good), 나쁘다 (nappeuda, bad), 상하다 (sanghada, to be hurt/damaged).
--- word ---
term: 나쁠래
type: verb ending
breakdown: 나쁘다 + -을래
meaning: want to be bad / make bad
notes: 나쁘다 (nappeuda) means 'to be bad'. The -(으)ㄹ래 (-(eu)llae) ending is an informal expression of intention or a question asking about the listener's intention. When combined with '기분' (gibun), '기분 나쁘다' means 'to feel bad' or 'to be in a bad mood'. So, '기분 나쁠래?' can mean 'Do you want to feel bad?' or, as interpreted here, 'Do you want to make (my) mood bad?'
--- word ---
term: 그래?
type: interjection/adverb
breakdown: 그렇다 + -어?
meaning: is that so? / really?
notes: A common informal conversational filler or question. 그렇다 (geureota) means 'to be so' or 'to be like that'. Adding the informal -어 (eo) ending and a rising intonation turns it into a question 'Is that so?' or 'Really?'. Here, it's used rhetorically, like 'Is that what you want to do?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ래?
meaning: Used informally to ask about the listener's intention or to express one's own intention. It can also be used to make a suggestion or a request.
example: 뭐 먹을래? (Mwo meogeullae?) - What do you want to eat?
level: Beginner

notes: 없다 (eopda) means 'to not exist' or 'to not have'. -었어 (eosseo) is the informal past tense ending. '정신이 없다' (jeongsin-i eopda) is an idiom meaning 'to be out of one's mind', 'to be distracted', or 'to be extremely busy'.
--- word ---
term: 가지고
type: verb ending
breakdown: 가지다 + -고
meaning: having (so), because of
notes: The full form is -아/어 가지고 (-a/eo gajigo), which is a colloquial connective ending indicating a reason or cause, similar to -아/어서 (-a/eoseo). It implies 'having done X, therefore Y' or 'because of X, Y happened'. Here, it means 'because I was so busy/distracted'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: N + 때문에
meaning: Indicates the reason or cause for an action or state. It attaches directly to nouns.
example: 교통 체증 때문에 늦었어요. (Gyotong chejeung ttaemune neujeosseoyo.) - I was late because of traffic congestion.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: 정신이 없다
meaning: An idiom meaning 'to be out of one's mind', 'to be distracted', 'to be extremely busy', or 'to have a lot on one's plate'.
example: 시험 기간이라 정신이 없어요. (Siheom gigan-ira jeongsin-i eopseoyo.) - I'm so busy because it's exam period.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 가지고
meaning: A colloquial connective ending that indicates a reason or cause, similar to -아/어서. It often implies a sequence of events where the first action leads to the second.
example: 배고파 가지고 밥 먹었어요. (Baegopa gajigo bap meogeosseoyo.) - I was hungry, so I ate.
level: Intermediate

=== [31:13] 연습도 그렇고 많은 일이 이번에 너무 겹쳐서 정신이 없었는데 ===
meaning: Practice too, and many things overlapped this time, so I was so busy/distracted.
tone: Informal, explanatory, slightly apologetic.
--- word ---
term: 연습도
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 연습 + -도
meaning: practice (also)
notes: 연습 (yeonseup) means 'practice'. -도 (do) is the additive particle meaning 'also' or 'too'. It implies that practice was one of the things that contributed to her being busy.
--- word ---
term: 그렇고
type: verb ending
breakdown: 그렇다 + -고
meaning: be like that, and
notes: 그렇다 (geureota) means 'to be so' or 'to be like that'. The -고 (go) ending connects clauses, meaning 'and'. 'X도 그렇고 Y도 그렇고' is a common pattern meaning 'X too, and Y too' or 'both X and Y'.
--- word ---
term: 많은
type: adjective
breakdown: 많다 + -은
meaning: many
notes: 많다 (manta) is the descriptive verb 'to be many/much'. -은 (eun) is the adjective-forming ending for descriptive verbs ending in a consonant (or for verbs ending in a vowel, -ㄴ). It modifies the following noun '일' (il, things).
--- word ---
term: 일이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 일 + -이
meaning: things, work (subject particle)
notes: 일 (il) means 'work', 'task', or 'thing/matter'. -이 (i) is the subject particle.
--- word ---
term: 이번에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 이번 + -에
meaning: this time
notes: 이번 (ibeon) means 'this time' or 'this occasion'. -에 (e) is a time/location particle.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: too, very, excessively
notes: Emphasizes the degree of overlapping. See notes for '너무' in segment 4.
--- word ---
term: 겹쳐서
type: verb ending
breakdown: 겹치다 + -어서
meaning: overlapped (so), because overlapped
notes: 겹치다 (gyeopchida) means 'to overlap' or 'to pile up'. The -어서 (eoseo) ending indicates a reason or cause, meaning 'because' or 'so'. So, 'because many things overlapped'.
--- word ---
term: 정신이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 정신 + -이
meaning: mind, spirit (subject particle)
notes: Part of the idiom '정신이 없다'. See notes for '정신이' in segment 4.
--- word ---
term: 없었는데
type: verb ending
breakdown: 없다 + -었 + -는데
meaning: was not (but/and)
notes: 없다 (eopda) means 'to not exist/have'. -었 (eot) is the past tense marker. -는데 (neunde) is a connective ending that provides background information, sets the scene, or introduces a mild contrast. Here, it sets the background for the next statement (implied: 'so I couldn't do X' or 'but now I'm doing Y').
--- grammar ---
pattern: A-도 그렇고 B-도 그렇고
meaning: Used to list multiple similar situations or items, meaning 'both A and B' or 'A too, and B too'.
example: 날씨도 그렇고 기분도 그렇고 다 좋아요. (Nalssido geureoko gibundo geureoko da johayo.) - The weather is good, my mood is good, everything is good.
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어서
meaning: Connects two clauses, indicating that the first clause is the reason or cause for the second clause. It means 'because' or 'so'.
example: 피곤해서 일찍 잤어요. (Pigonhaeseo iljjik jasseoyo.) - I was tired, so I went to bed early.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는데/은데
meaning: Provides background information, sets the scene for the following statement, or indicates a mild contrast or expectation. It can be translated as 'but', 'and', 'so', or 'while'.
example: 비가 오는데 우산이 없어요. (Biga oneunde usani eopseoyo.) - It's raining, but I don't have an umbrella.
level: Beginner

=== [31:20] 조만간 또 빨리 놀러 올게 알았지? ===
meaning: I'll come visit again soon, okay?
tone: Informal, friendly, reassuring, seeking confirmation.
--- word ---
term: 조만간
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: soon, in the near future
notes: Indicates that something will happen in a short period of time. Similar to 곧 (got).
--- word ---
term: 또
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again
notes: Indicates repetition. See notes for '또' in segment 3.
--- word ---
term: 빨리
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: quickly, fast
notes: Modifies the verb '놀러 올게' (nolleo olge), emphasizing that she will come quickly.
--- word ---
term: 놀러
type: verb ending
breakdown: 놀다 + -러
meaning: to play, to visit (for fun)
notes: 놀다 (nolda) means 'to play' or 'to hang out'. The -러 (reo) ending indicates purpose, meaning 'in order to' or 'for the purpose of'. '놀러 오다' (nolleo oda) means 'to come to visit (for fun)' or 'to come hang out'.
--- word ---
term: 올게
type: verb ending
breakdown: 오다 + -ㄹ게
meaning: will come (promise/intention)
notes: 오다 (oda) means 'to come'. The -(으)ㄹ게 (-(eu)lge) ending expresses the speaker's intention or promise to do something for the listener or in consideration of the listener. It's a polite but informal promise.
--- word ---
term: 알았지?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 알다 + -았지?
meaning: understood, right?
notes: 알다 (alda) means 'to know' or 'to understand'. -았지 (-atji) is the informal past tense ending combined with -지 (-ji), which is used to seek confirmation or agreement. So, '알았지?' means 'You understood, right?' or 'Okay?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -러/으러 가다/오다
meaning: Indicates the purpose of going or coming, meaning 'to go/come in order to...' or 'to go/come for the purpose of...'.
example: 밥 먹으러 가요. (Bap meogeureo gayo.) - Let's go eat.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ게
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or promise to do something, often for the benefit of the listener or with the listener in mind. It implies a commitment.
example: 제가 도와줄게요. (Jega dowajulgeyo.) - I will help you.
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지?
meaning: Attached to the end of a sentence to ask for confirmation or agreement from the listener. It implies that the speaker expects the listener to agree.
example: 이거 맛있지? (Igeo masitji?) - This is delicious, isn't it?
level: Beginner

=== [31:24] 도넛 잘 먹을게 고마워 ===
meaning: I'll eat the donuts well. Thank you.
tone: Informal, appreciative, concluding.
--- word ---
term: 도넛
type: noun
breakdown: 
meaning: donut
notes: A loanword from English, referring to the pastry 'donut'. The STORY BIBLE mentions Hwasa thanking fans for donuts.
--- word ---
term: 잘
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: well
notes: An adverb meaning 'well' or 'properly'. Here, it implies she will enjoy eating them thoroughly.
--- word ---
term: 먹을게
type: verb ending
breakdown: 먹다 + -을게
meaning: will eat (promise/intention)
notes: 먹다 (meokda) means 'to eat'. The -(으)ㄹ게 (-(eu)lge) ending expresses a promise or intention. Hwasa is promising her fans that she will eat the donuts they sent her. See notes for '-(으)ㄹ게' in segment 6.
--- word ---
term: 고마워
type: adjective
breakdown: 고맙다 + -어
meaning: thank you (informal)
notes: 고맙다 (gomapda) is a descriptive verb meaning 'to be thankful'. The -어 (eo) ending is the informal, casual speech ending. This is a common informal way to say 'thank you'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ게
meaning: Expresses the speaker's intention or promise to do something, often for the benefit of the listener or with the listener in mind. It implies a commitment.
example: 제가 도와줄게요. (Jega dowajulgeyo.) - I will help you.
level: Beginner

=== [31:28] 그리고 건강 행복 안녕 ===
meaning: And health, happiness, and well-being.
tone: Informal, warm, and concluding, offering good wishes to the audience.
--- word ---
term: 그리고
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 그리고
meaning: And, then
notes: Used to connect words, phrases, or clauses. It can mean 'and' when connecting nouns or verbs, or 'then' when connecting sequential actions or ideas. It's a versatile conjunction used in both formal and informal contexts.
--- word ---
term: 건강
type: Noun
breakdown: 건강
meaning: Health
notes: This noun refers to 'health'. It's commonly used in well-wishes, often paired with '행복' (happiness) and '안녕' (well-being/peace) to express a comprehensive wish for someone's welfare. For example, '건강하세요' (geon-gang-ha-se-yo) means 'Please be healthy' (formal).
--- word ---
term: 행복
type: Noun
breakdown: 행복
meaning: Happiness
notes: This noun means 'happiness'. Like '건강' (health), it's frequently used in greetings and farewells to express a wish for someone's happiness. For instance, '행복하세요' (haeng-bok-ha-se-yo) means 'Please be happy' (formal).
--- word ---
term: 안녕
type: Noun / Interjection
breakdown: 안녕
meaning: Well-being, peace
notes: While '안녕' is widely known as the informal greeting 'hello' or 'goodbye', its core meaning is 'peace' or 'well-being'. In this context, following '건강' (health) and '행복' (happiness), it functions as a noun expressing a wish for overall 'well-being' or 'peace', completing a series of good wishes. It's a common way to express general good wishes in a concise manner, especially when concluding a conversation or broadcast.
